At the fine stone house, with its green-jalousied windows, which M. d’Ogeron had built himself in a spacious and luxuriant garden to the east of Cayona, the Captain became a very welcome guest. M. d’Ogeron was in the Captain’s debt for more than the twenty thousand pieces of eight which he had provided for mademoiselle’s ransom; and shrewd, hard bargain-driver though he might be, the Frenchman could be generous and understood the sentiment of gratitude. This he now proved in every possible way, and under his powerful protection the credit of Captain Blood among the buccaneers very rapidly reached its zenith.
So when it came to fitting out his fleet for that enterprise against Maracaybo, which had originally been Levasseur’s project, he did not want for either ships or men to follow him. He recruited five hundred adventurers in all, and he might have had as many thousands if he could have offered them accommodation. Similarly without difficulty he might have increased his fleet to twice its strength of ships but that he preferred to keep it what it was. The three vessels to which he confined it were the *Arabella*, the *La Foudre*, which Cahusac now commanded with a contingent of some sixscore Frenchmen, and the *Santiago*, which had been refitted and rechristened the *Elizabeth*, after that Queen of England whose seamen had humbled Spain as Captain Blood now hoped to humble it again. Hagthorpe, in virtue of his service in the navy, was appointed by Blood to command her, and the appointment was confirmed by the men.
It was some months after the rescue of Mademoiselle d’Ogeron﻿—in August of that year 1687﻿—that this little fleet, after some minor adventures which I pass over in silence, sailed into the great lake of Maracaybo and effected its raid upon that opulent city of the Main.
The affair did not proceed exactly as was hoped, and Blood’s force came to find itself in a precarious position. This is best explained in the words employed by Cahusac﻿—which Pitt has carefully recorded﻿—in the course of an altercation that broke out on the steps of the Church of Nuestra Señora del Carmen, which Captain Blood had impiously appropriated for the purpose of a corps-de-garde.

===Chinese===

德奥杰龙小姐那一桩事，自然结出了果实：布拉德船长同托尔图加总督之间本已亲厚的关系，更上了一层。德奥杰龙先生在卡约纳以东一座宽敞葱茏的花园里，为自己盖了一所漂亮的石头宅子，窗上装着绿色百叶；船长成了这宅子里极受欢迎的客人。欠船长的，除他为小姐赎身出的那两万枚八里亚尔，德奥杰龙先生还欠着更多；这位法国人尽管又精明又狠，却也慷慨得起来，也懂得感恩的情分。这一点他如今想尽法子来证明；有他有力的庇护，布拉德船长在海盗中的威望，飞快登上了绝顶。
于是，等到他为那桩——原是勒瓦瑟尔的图谋——进袭马拉开波的大业置办舰队时，跟着他干的船和人都不愁。他招募了五百名冒险家；只要装得下，几千人他也招得到。同样，把舰队添上一倍也不难，只是他宁肯维持原样。他限定三条船：“阿拉贝拉号”；如今由卡于萨克带着约一百二十名法国人统领的“拉富德雷号”；还有修缮一新、重新命名为“伊丽莎白号”（*Elizabeth*）的“圣地亚哥号”——取的是那位英格兰女王的名号，她的水手们曾叫西班牙折过腰，布拉德船长如今正指望再折它一回。哈格索普凭着他在海军里当过差的资历，由布拉德任命为该舰舰长，部下也一致认可。
救出德奥杰龙小姐几个月之后——一六八七年八月——这支小舰队，在几桩我略过不提的小小冒险之后，驶进了马拉开波大湖，对大陆上这座富庶的城市下手了。
事情的进展同指望的不尽相同，布拉德的部伍渐渐发觉自己处境堪危。这一点，用卡于萨克的话说最明白——皮特细心记下了——那是在卡门圣母教堂台阶上爆发的一场争吵里说的；布拉德船长不敬神明，把这教堂征用了当卫兵室。

===Original===

I have said already that he was a papist only when it suited him.
The dispute was being conducted by Hagthorpe, Wolverstone, and Pitt on the one side, and Cahusac, out of whose uneasiness it all arose, on the other. Behind them in the sun-scorched, dusty square, sparsely fringed by palms, whose fronds drooped listlessly in the quivering heat, surged a couple of hundred wild fellows belonging to both parties, their own excitement momentarily quelled so that they might listen to what passed among their leaders.
Cahusac appeared to be having it all his own way, and he raised his harsh, querulous voice so that all might hear his truculent denunciation. He spoke, Pitt tells us, a dreadful kind of English, which the shipmaster, however, makes little attempt to reproduce. His dress was as discordant as his speech. It was of a kind to advertise his trade, and ludicrously in contrast with the sober garb of Hagthorpe and the almost foppish daintiness of Jeremy Pitt. His soiled and bloodstained shirt of blue cotton was open in front, to cool his hairy breast, and the girdle about the waist of his leather breeches carried an arsenal of pistols and a knife, whilst a cutlass hung from a leather baldrick loosely slung about his body; above his countenance, broad and flat as a Mongolian’s, a red scarf was swathed, turban-wise, about his head.
“Is it that I have not warned you from the beginning that all was too easy?” he demanded between plaintiveness and fury. “I am no fool, my friends. I have eyes, me. And I see. I see an abandoned fort at the entrance of the lake, and nobody there to fire a gun at us when we came in. Then I suspect the trap. Who would not that had eyes and brain? Bah! we come on. What do we find? A city, abandoned like the fort; a city out of which the people have taken all things of value. Again I warn Captain Blood. It is a trap, I say. We are to come on; always to come on, without opposition, until we find that it is too late to go to sea again, that we cannot go back at all. But no one will listen to me. You all know so much more. Name of God! Captain Blood, he will go on, and we go on. We go to Gibraltar. True that at last, after long time, we catch the Deputy-Governor; true, we make him pay big ransom for Gibraltar; true between that ransom and the loot we return here with some two thousand pieces of eight.

===Chinese===

我早说过，他的天主教徒，只当得合他心意的时候。
争吵的一方是哈格索普、伍尔夫斯通和皮特，另一方是卡于萨克——这场不安本就出自他。他们身后，在被太阳烤焦的、尘土飞扬的广场上——广场边上稀稀落落立着几株棕榈，羽叶在颤动的热浪里有气无力地垂着——涌动着两三百条两方面的野汉子，他们自己的亢奋一时按下了，好听听头领们谈出什么结果。
卡于萨克俨然占了全盘上风，他提高那副又吵又怨的嗓门，好叫人人都听见他那挑衅的指斥。据皮特说，他操一口可怕的英语，好在船长没怎么费心去照录。他的衣着同他的言语一样不搭调，一看便知是干哪一行的，同哈格索普的素净衣装、杰里米·皮特近于花花公子的考究，形成了滑稽的对照。他那件蓝布衬衫又脏又带血渍，敞着怀，好让毛茸茸的胸口凉快；皮马裤腰带上插着一整套手枪和一把刀，一条皮肩带松松挎着，挂一柄弯刀；那张又宽又扁、活像蒙古人的脸膛上头，一条红巾裹着头，裹成了头巾帽。
“打头一起，我就警告你们，事事太顺，难道是我没警告？”他又怨又怒地质问。“我不是傻子，朋友们。我有眼睛，我。我看得见。我看见湖口一座弃守的堡垒，咱们进来时没一个人放一炮。我就疑心是陷阱。有眼睛有脑子的，谁不疑心？呸！咱们照旧往前。看见了什么？一座城，同堡垒一样弃了；一座城里，值钱的东西叫人搬了个精光。我又警告布拉德船长。我说，这是陷阱。要咱们往前；一直往前，没人拦，直到发觉再出海已经晚了，退也退不回。可谁也不听我的。你们个个都懂得多。上帝之名！布拉德船长，他偏要往前，咱们就往前。咱们去了直布罗陀。不错，末了，过了好一阵，抓到了副督；不错，叫他为直布罗陀付了大笔赎金；不错，那笔赎金加上掳获，咱们带着约莫两千枚八里亚尔回到这儿。

===Original===

But what is it, in reality, will you tell me? Or shall I tell you? It is a piece of cheese﻿—a piece of cheese in a mousetrap, and we are the little mice. Goddam! And the cats﻿—oh, the cats they wait for us! The cats are those four Spanish ships of war that have come meantime. And they wait for us outside the bottleneck of this lagoon. *Mort de Dieu!* That is what comes of the damned obstinacy of your fine Captain Blood.”
Wolverstone laughed. Cahusac exploded in fury.
“Ah, *sangdieu*! *Tu ris*, animal? You laugh! Tell me this: How do we get out again unless we accept the terms of Monsieur the Admiral of Spain?”
From the buccaneers at the foot of the steps came an angry rumble of approval. The single eye of the gigantic Wolverstone rolled terribly, and he clenched his great fists as if to strike the Frenchman, who was exposing them to mutiny. But Cahusac was not daunted. The mood of the men enheartened him.
“You think, perhaps, this your Captain Blood is the good God. That he can make miracles, eh? He is ridiculous, you know, this Captain Blood; with his grand air and his﻿ ﻿…”
He checked. Out of the church at that moment, grand air and all, sauntered Peter Blood. With him came a tough, long-legged French sea-wolf named Yberville, who, though still young, had already won fame as a privateer commander before the loss of his own ship had driven him to take service under Blood. The Captain advanced towards that disputing group, leaning lightly upon his long ebony cane, his face shaded by a broad-plumed hat. There was in his appearance nothing of the buccaneer. He had much more the air of a lounger in the Mall or the Alameda﻿—the latter rather, since his elegant suit of violet taffetas with gold-embroidered buttonholes was in the Spanish fashion. But the long, stout, serviceable rapier, thrust up behind by the left hand resting lightly on the pummel, corrected the impression. That and those steely eyes of his announced the adventurer.
“You find me ridiculous, eh, Cahusac?” said he, as he came to a halt before the Breton, whose anger seemed already to have gone out of him. “What, then, must I find you?” He spoke quietly, almost wearily. “You will be telling them that we have delayed, and that it is the delay that has brought about our danger. But whose is the fault of that delay?

===Chinese===

可这算什么，说到底？要我说，还是你们说？这是一块奶酪——耗子夹上的一块奶酪，咱们就是那几只小耗子。天杀的！还有猫——哦，猫正等着咱们呢！猫就是这几个星期里来的那四条西班牙战船。它们就在这泻湖瓶颈外头等着咱们。天主死亡！这就是你们那位好船长布拉德那该死的固执的下场。”
伍尔夫斯通笑了。卡于萨克勃然大怒。
“啊，血神！你笑，畜生？你笑！那你告诉我：不接受西班牙提督大人的条件，咱们怎么再出去？”
台阶脚下的海盗群里响起一阵怒冲冲的附和。巨人伍尔夫斯通那只独眼可怕地转着，攥紧两只大拳，像要揍这法国人——他这是在煽动他们哗变。可卡于萨克不怕。部下的情绪给他壮了胆。
“你们怕是以为，你们这位布拉德船长就是上帝本尊，会显神通，嗯？他可笑得很呢，这位布拉德船长；那副大架子，还有他那……”
他打住了。就在那一刻，那位大架子本人，彼得·布拉德，从教堂里踱了出来。随他一起的是个瘦长精悍的法国海狼，名叫伊贝维尔（Yberville）——此人年纪虽轻，却早挣下了私掠船长的名声，后来自己的船丢了，才投到布拉德麾下听命。船长朝那群争论的人走去，轻轻拄着一根乌木长手杖，一顶插着阔羽毛的帽子遮着脸。他这一身，没有半点海盗气，倒更像圣詹姆斯林荫道或阿拉梅达林荫道上的闲人——宁可说后者，因为他那身紫罗兰色塔夫绸、金绣扣眼的漂亮衣裳是西班牙式样。可那柄又长又结实、堪堪好用的细剑，由左手虚搭着剑柄，从身后斜挑着，纠正了这印象。这一样，加上他那双钢一样的眼睛，宣告了冒险家的身份。
“你当我很可笑，嗯，卡于萨克？”他在布列塔尼人面前站定，说。对方的怒气像是先自泄了。“那我又该当你是什么呢？”他说得平静，近乎疲倦。“你正要告诉他们：我们耽搁了，正是耽搁招来了眼下的危险。可这耽搁，是谁的过错？

===Original===

We have been a month in doing what should have been done, and what but for your blundering would have been done, inside of a week.”
“*Ah ça! Nom de Dieu!* Was it my fault that﻿ ﻿…”
“Was it anyone else’s fault that you ran your ship *La Foudre* aground on the shoal in the middle of the lake? You would not be piloted. You knew your way. You took no soundings even. The result was that we lost three precious days in getting canoes to bring off your men and your gear. Those three days gave the folk at Gibraltar not only time to hear of our coming, but time in which to get away. After that, and because of it, we had to follow the Governor to his infernal island fortress, and a fortnight and best part of a hundred lives were lost in reducing it. That’s how we come to have delayed until this Spanish fleet is fetched round from La Guayra by a *guarda-costa*; and if ye hadn’t lost *La Foudre*, and so reduced our fleet from three ships to two, we should even now be able to fight our way through with a reasonable hope of succeeding. Yet you think it is for you to come hectoring here, upbraiding us for a situation that is just the result of your own ineptitude.”
He spoke with a restraint which I trust you will agree was admirable when I tell you that the Spanish fleet guarding the bottleneck exit of the great Lake of Maracaybo, and awaiting there the coming forth of Captain Blood with a calm confidence based upon its overwhelming strength, was commanded by his implacable enemy, Don Miguel de Espinosa y Valdez, the Admiral of Spain. In addition to his duty to his country, the Admiral had, as you know, a further personal incentive arising out of that business aboard the *Encarnación* a year ago, and the death of his brother Don Diego; and with him sailed his nephew Esteban, whose vindictive zeal exceeded the Admiral’s own.
Yet, knowing all this, Captain Blood could preserve his calm in reproving the cowardly frenzy of one for whom the situation had not half the peril with which it was fraught for himself. He turned from Cahusac to address the mob of buccaneers, who had surged nearer to hear him, for he had not troubled to raise his voice. “I hope that will correct some of the misapprehension that appears to have been disturbing you,” said he.
“There’s no good can come of talking of what’s past and done,” cried Cahusac, more sullen now than truculent.

===Chinese===

本该一星期里办完的事，我们办了一个月——若不是你坏事，本就一星期办完了。”
“啊，这！上帝之名！难道是我的错，我……”
“你的船‘拉富德雷号’在湖心浅滩上搁了浅，难道是别人的错？让你领航你不肯。你认得路。你连测深也不测。结果，我们白白搭进去三天宝贵的工夫，调独木船去接你的人马家伙。就这三天，叫直布罗陀的人不但得了咱们要来的信儿，还得了跑路的工夫。此后，也正为这个，我们只得追总督追到他那座鬼岛堡垒，攻下它又赔上半个月工夫和近百条人命。我们就这样耽搁下来，直到西班牙舰队由一条缉私船从拉瓜伊拉接了来，堵在湖口。倘若你没丢‘拉富德雷号’、没把舰队从三条减成两条，我们此刻原还能杀出去，赢面也像样。如今你倒觉得，该由你上这儿来逞威风，把我们骂作茧自缚——这局面，正是你自己无能的结果。”
他说这番话时的克制，我想诸位会同意实属可贵——待我告诉你：把守马拉开博大湖瓶颈出口、以压倒的实力为后盾、安坐待布拉德船长出来的西班牙舰队，统帅正是他不共戴天的仇人、西班牙提督堂米格尔·德·埃斯皮诺萨-伊-巴尔德斯。除了对国家的职分，提督还有一层——诸位知道——出自一年前“恩卡纳西翁号”上那桩事和他弟弟堂迭戈之死的私怨；随他出航的还有他侄子埃斯特万，那复仇的心火，比提督本人烧得还旺。
然而明知这一切，布拉德船长斥责这个懦夫的慌乱时，仍能保持平静——对这人而言，处境的危险还不及他本人所面对的一半。他离开卡于萨克，对围拢过来听他说话的海盗群讲话——他懒得提高嗓门。“我希望，这能纠正一些看来一直在搅扰你们的误会，”他说。
“过去的、成了的事，谈也无益，”卡于萨克叫道，此刻与其说凶横，不如说阴沉了。

===Original===

Whereupon Wolverstone laughed, a laugh that was like the neighing of a horse. “The question is: what are we to do now?”
“Sure, now, there’s no question at all,” said Captain Blood.
“Indeed, but there is,” Cahusac insisted. “Don Miguel, the Spanish Admiral, have offer us safe passage to sea if we will depart at once, do no damage to the town, release our prisoners, and surrender all that we took at Gibraltar.”
Captain Blood smiled quietly, knowing precisely how much Don Miguel’s word was worth. It was Yberville who replied, in manifest scorn of his compatriot:
“Which argues that, even at this disadvantage as he has us, the Spanish Admiral is still afraid of us.”
“That can be only because he not know our real weakness,” was the fierce retort. “And, anyway, we must accept these terms. We have no choice. That is my opinion.”
“Well, it’s not mine, now,” said Captain Blood. “So, I’ve refused them.”
“Refuse’!” Cahusac’s broad face grew purple. A muttering from the men behind enheartened him. “You have refuse’? You have refuse’ already﻿—and without consulting me?”
“Your disagreement could have altered nothing. You’d have been outvoted, for Hagthorpe here was entirely of my own mind. Still,” he went on, “if you and your own French followers wish to avail yourselves of the Spaniard’s terms, we shall not hinder you. Send one of your prisoners to announce it to the Admiral. Don Miguel will welcome your decision, you may be sure.”
Cahusac glowered at him in silence for a moment. Then, having controlled himself, he asked in a concentrated voice:
“Precisely what answer have you make to the Admiral?”
A smile irradiated the face and eyes of Captain Blood. “I have answered him that unless within four-and-twenty hours we have his parole to stand out to sea, ceasing to dispute our passage or hinder our departure, and a ransom of fifty thousand pieces of eight for Maracaybo, we shall reduce this beautiful city to ashes, and thereafter go out and destroy his fleet.”
The impudence of it left Cahusac speechless. But among the English buccaneers in the square there were many who savoured the audacious humour of the trapped dictating terms to the trappers. Laughter broke from them. It spread into a roar of acclamation; for bluff is a weapon dear to every adventurer.

===Chinese===

伍尔夫斯通闻言笑了，那笑像马嘶。“问题是：现在怎么办？”
“说真的，现在，根本不成问题，”布拉德船长说。
“恰恰相反，有得是，”卡于萨克坚持。“堂米格尔，西班牙提督，开了条件：咱们即刻撤离、不损城镇、放还俘虏、交出直布罗陀所得的一切，他就放咱们平安出海。”
布拉德船长淡淡一笑——堂米格尔的话值几个钱，他一清二楚。答话的是伊贝维尔，对他的同胞满是不屑：
“这倒说明，纵然占了这等便宜，西班牙提督还是怕咱们。”
“那只能是因为他不摸咱们的真正短处，”对方狠狠回敬。“反正，咱们非接受这些条件不可。咱们没有别的路。这是我的意见。”
“哦，可不是我的，”布拉德船长说。“所以，我回绝了。”
“回绝了！”卡于萨克的大宽脸涨成紫色。身后的人群一阵嘨咕，给他壮了胆。“你回绝了？你已经回绝了——也不同我商量？”
“你不赞成也改变不了什么。你会被多数压倒，因为哈格索普同我完全一条心。不过，”他接着说，“你和你那班法国部下若想领受西班牙人的条件，我们不为难你们。派你们一个俘虏去报与提督。堂米格尔必欢迎你们这个决定，你只管放心。”
卡于萨克一言不发地瞪了他半晌。随后，强自按捺住了，他声音发紧地问：
“你到底给了提督什么答复？”
一丝笑照亮了布拉德船长的脸和眼睛。“我答复他：二十四小时内，他若不给出字据、退出外海，不再阻拦咱们的航路和去留，并献上五万枚八里亚尔赎马拉开波，我们就把这座漂亮城池烧成白地，然后出去，把他的舰队报销。”
这份胆气堵得卡于萨克说不出话。可广场上的英国海盗里，许多人品出了这股狂妄的谐趣——落网的给下套的开条件。笑声从他们中间炸开，漫成一片叫好；虚张声势本就是冒险家们人人珍爱的兵器。

===Original===

Presently, when they understood it, even Cahusac’s French followers were carried off their feet by that wave of jocular enthusiasm, until in his truculent obstinacy Cahusac remained the only dissentient. He withdrew in mortification. Nor was he to be mollified until the following day brought him his revenge. This came in the shape of a messenger from Don Miguel with a letter in which the Spanish Admiral solemnly vowed to God that, since the pirates had refused his magnanimous offer to permit them to surrender with the honours of war, he would now await them at the mouth of the lake there to destroy them on their coming forth. He added that should they delay their departure, he would so soon as he was reinforced by a fifth ship, the *Santo Niño*, on its way to join him from La Guayra, himself come inside to seek them at Maracaybo.
This time Captain Blood was put out of temper.
“Trouble me no more,” he snapped at Cahusac, who came growling to him again. “Send word to Don Miguel that you have seceded from me. He’ll give you safe conduct, devil a doubt. Then take one of the sloops, order your men aboard and put to sea, and the devil go with you.”
Cahusac would certainly have adopted that course if only his men had been unanimous in the matter. They, however, were torn between greed and apprehension. If they went they must abandon their share of the plunder, which was considerable, as well as the slaves and other prisoners they had taken. If they did this, and Captain Blood should afterwards contrive to get away unscathed﻿—and from their knowledge of his resourcefulness, the thing, however unlikely, need not be impossible﻿—he must profit by that which they now relinquished. This was a contingency too bitter for contemplation. And so, in the end, despite all that Cahusac could say, the surrender was not to Don Miguel, but to Peter Blood. They had come into the venture with him, they asserted, and they would go out of it with him or not at all. That was the message he received from them that same evening by the sullen mouth of Cahusac himself.
He welcomed it, and invited the Breton to sit down and join the council which was even then deliberating upon the means to be employed.

===Chinese===

不一刻，待他们咂摸过味来，连卡于萨克的法国部下也叫这阵快活的狂热卷了进去，到最后，凶顽执拗的卡于萨克成了惟一的异议者。他悻悻地退了出去。直到第二天他的雪耻到了，他这才回慎作喜。雪耻的形状，是堂米格尔派来的一名信使和一封信：提督在信里对天起誓，海盗们既回绝了他许他们带战争荣光投降的宽宏大度，他如今就在湖口候着，待他们出来时把他们消灭。他还说，他们若迟迟不走，等他从拉瓜伊拉来会的第五条船“圣尼诺号”（*Santo Niño*）一到，他便亲自进湖，到马拉开波来寻他们。
这一回，布拉德船长恼了。
“别再来搅我，”他冲又来咆哮的卡于萨克呵斥道。“给堂米格尔送个信，说你已同我分道。他必给你通行证，包管错不了。然后领一条单桅船，带上你的人出海去，叫魔鬼随你去。”
卡于萨克原一定会走这条路，只消他的人齐心。可他们叫贪心和惶恐两头撕扯。走了，就得撚下他们那份掳获——为数可观——连同抓来的奴隶和别的俘虏。倘若撚下了，而布拉德船长事后竟又全身而退——凭他们对他的急智的了解，这事再不可能也不是全无可能——那他们此刻撒手的东西，就全便宜了他。这前景苦得没法想。于是到头来，不顾卡于萨克百般撞掇，他们投降的不是堂米格尔，而是彼得·布拉德。他们说，这桩买卖是同他入的伙，出去也非同他一道不可，不然宁可不出。这便是当晚他们借卡于萨克那张阴沉的嘴，转给他的话。
他欢迎这话，还请这布列塔尼人坐下，加入正在计议方略的会议。

===Original===

This council occupied the spacious patio of the Governor’s house﻿—which Captain Blood had appropriated to his own uses﻿—a cloistered stone quadrangle in the middle of which a fountain played coolly under a trellis of vine. Orange-trees grew on two sides of it, and the still, evening air was heavy with the scent of them. It was one of those pleasant exterior-interiors which Moorish architects had introduced to Spain and the Spaniards had carried with them to the New World.
Here that council of war, composed of six men in all, deliberated until late that night upon the plan of action which Captain Blood put forward.
The great freshwater lake of Maracaybo, nourished by a score of rivers from the snow-capped ranges that surround it on two sides, is some hundred and twenty miles in length and almost the same distance across at its widest. It is﻿—as has been indicated﻿—in the shape of a great bottle having its neck towards the sea at Maracaybo.
Beyond this neck it widens again, and then the two long, narrow strips of land known as the islands of Vigilias and Palomas block the channel, standing lengthwise across it. The only passage out to sea for vessels of any draught lies in the narrow strait between these islands. Palomas, which is some ten miles in length, is unapproachable for half a mile on either side by any but the shallowest craft save at its eastern end, where, completely commanding the narrow passage out to sea, stands the massive fort which the buccaneers had found deserted upon their coming. In the broader water between this passage and the bar, the four Spanish ships were at anchor in mid-channel. The Admiral’s *Encarnación*, which we already know, was a mighty galleon of forty-eight great guns and eight small. Next in importance was the *Salvador* with thirty-six guns; the other two, the *Infanta* and the *San Felipe*, though smaller vessels, were still formidable enough with their twenty guns and a hundred and fifty men apiece.
Such was the fleet of which the gauntlet was to be run by Captain Blood with his own *Arabella* of forty guns, the *Elizabeth* of twenty-six, and two sloops captured at Gibraltar, which they had indifferently armed with four culverins each. In men they had a bare four hundred survivors of the five hundred-odd that had left Tortuga, to oppose to fully a thousand Spaniards manning the galleons.

===Chinese===

会场占了总督府宽敞的天井——布拉德船长已把总督府挪作己用——四合的石廊环着，正中一泓泉水在葡萄棚下清凉地喷洒。两侧栽着橘树，傍晚静定的空气沉沉地含着橘花香。这是摩尔匠人带入西班牙、又被西班牙人携来新大陆的那种惬意的“露天内庭”。
军事会议一共六人，就着布拉德船长提出的行动计划，计议到深夜。
马拉开波大湖是淡水湖，由两侧雪山岭脉泻下的二十来条河流滋养，长约一百二十英里，最宽处也几乎这数。如前所示，它形似一只大瓶，瓶颈朝海，就在马拉开波城外。
瓶颈之外湖面又阔，随即两条狭长的陆地——所谓维希利亚斯（Vigilias）与帕洛马斯（Palomas）诸岛——纵向横亘水道。凡有些吃水的船，出海的惟一通路，就在两岛之间那条窄峡。帕洛马斯岛长约十英里，两侧各半英里内，除了最浅的船，什么都近不得，只东端例外——就在那里，把着出海窄口，立着那座敦实的大堡垒，海盗们初来时发现它空无一人。在这条通路和沙洲之间较宽的水面上，四条西班牙船泊在航道正中。提督的“恩卡纳西翁号”——我们已经认识——是一条四十八门重炮、八门轻炮的巍峨大帆船。次一等的是三十六门炮的“萨尔瓦多号”（*Salvador*）；另两条“因凡塔号”（*Infanta*）和“圣费利佩号”（*San Felipe*）虽小些，也各有二十门炮、一百五十人，仍然不好惹。
布拉德船长要闯的这道关，对方是这样一支舰队；他手里，是他那条四十门炮的“阿拉贝拉号”、二十六门炮的“伊丽莎白号”，外加在直布罗陀掳来的两条单桅船——马马虎虎各装了四门长炮。人数上，离了托尔图加时的五百出头，如今只剩区区四百，对面大帆船上足足一千西班牙人。

===Original===

The plan of action submitted by Captain Blood to that council was a desperate one, as Cahusac uncompromisingly pronounced it.
“Why, so it is,” said the Captain. “But I’ve done things more desperate.” Complacently he pulled at a pipe that was loaded with that fragrant Sacerdotes tobacco for which Gibraltar was famous, and of which they had brought away some hogsheads. “And what is more, they’ve succeeded. *Audaces fortuna juvat.* Bedad, they knew their world, the old Romans.”
He breathed into his companions and even into Cahusac some of his own spirit of confidence, and in confidence all went busily to work. For three days from sunrise to sunset, the buccaneers laboured and sweated to complete the preparations for the action that was to procure them their deliverance. Time pressed. They must strike before Don Miguel de Espinosa received the reinforcement of that fifth galleon, the *Santo Niño*, which was coming to join him from La Guayra.
Their principal operations were on the larger of the two sloops captured at Gibraltar; to which vessel was assigned the leading part in Captain Blood’s scheme. They began by tearing down all bulkheads, until they had reduced her to the merest shell, and in her sides they broke open so many ports that her gunwale was converted into the semblance of a grating. Next they increased by a half-dozen the scuttles in her deck, whilst into her hull they packed all the tar and pitch and brimstone that they could find in the town, to which they added six barrels of gunpowder, placed on end like guns at the open ports on her larboard side.
On the evening of the fourth day, everything being now in readiness, all were got aboard, and the empty, pleasant city of Maracaybo was at last abandoned. But they did not weigh anchor until some two hours after midnight. Then, at last, on the first of the ebb, they drifted silently down towards the bar with all canvas furled save only their spritsails, which, so as to give them steering way, were spread to the faint breeze that stirred through the purple darkness of the tropical night.
The order of their going was as follows: Ahead went the improvised fire-ship in charge of Wolverstone, with a crew of six volunteers, each of whom was to have a hundred pieces of eight over and above his share of plunder as a special reward. Next came the *Arabella*.

===Chinese===

布拉德船长向会议提出的行动计划是个孤注一掷的计划，卡于萨克毫不含糊地这样断言。
“哦，正是，”船长说。“可更悬的我干过。”他怡然自得地抽着一斗烟，斗里装的是直布罗陀闻名的、芬芳的萨塞尔多特斯烟叶——他们带走了好几桶。“而且，都成了。天佑勇者。真的，老罗马人是懂得他们那个世界的。”
他把自己那份自信，注入同伴们、连卡于萨克的身体里；人人怀着自信，忙碌地干了起来。整整三天，从日出到日落，海盗们为置办那桩将救他们脱险的行动埋头流汗。时间紧迫。他们必须赶在堂米格尔·德·埃斯皮诺萨得到第五条大帆船“圣尼诺号”增援之前动手——那船正从拉瓜伊拉赶来同他会合。
主要的工程做在直布罗陀掳来的两条单桅船里较大的那条上；在布拉德船长的计划里，这条船担任主角。他们先拆光全部舱壁，把它拆到只剩一副空壳；又在船身上凿开多得数不清的炮眼，把舷墙弄成一面格棚的样子。接着把甲板上的舱口加了六个，往船壳里塞满全城寻得来的焦油、沥青、硫磺，再添六桶火药，像炮似的直立在左舷敞开的炮眼里。
第四天傍晚，一切齐备，全员上船，空荡荡、静悄悄的马拉开波城总算被撚下了。可直到午夜过后约两个钟头，他们才起锚。于是，趁着落潮的头一股，他们收拢了所有的帆，只张开斜桁帆，趁那一缕掠过热带之夜紫色幽暗的微风挣得舵效，悄没声地朝沙洲漂下去。
出发的序列如下：打头的是那条改装的火船，伍尔夫斯通管带，六名志愿的船手，每人除掳获分润外，另赏一百枚八里亚尔。随后是“阿拉贝拉号”。

===Original===

She was followed at a distance by the *Elizabeth*, commanded by Hagthorpe, with whom was the now shipless Cahusac and the bulk of his French followers. The rear was brought up by the second sloop and some eight canoes, aboard of which had been shipped the prisoners, the slaves, and most of the captured merchandise. The prisoners were all pinioned, and guarded by four buccaneers with musketoons who manned these boats in addition to the two fellows who were to sail them. Their place was to be in the rear and they were to take no part whatever in the coming fight.
As the first glimmerings of opalescent dawn dissolved the darkness, the straining eyes of the buccaneers were able to make out the tall rigging of the Spanish vessels, riding at anchor less than a quarter of a mile ahead. Entirely without suspicion as the Spaniards were, and rendered confident by their own overwhelming strength, it is unlikely that they used a vigilance keener than their careless habit. Certain it is that they did not sight Blood’s fleet in that dim light until some time after Blood’s fleet had sighted them. By the time that they had actively roused themselves, Wolverstone’s sloop was almost upon them, speeding under canvas which had been crowded to her yards the moment the galleons had loomed into view.
Straight for the Admiral’s great ship, the *Encarnación*, did Wolverstone head the sloop; then, lashing down the helm, he kindled from a match that hung ready lighted beside him a great torch of thickly plaited straw that had been steeped in bitumen. First it glowed, then as he swung it round his head, it burst into flame, just as the slight vessel went crashing and bumping and scraping against the side of the flagship, whilst rigging became tangled with rigging, to the straining of yards and snapping of spars overhead. His six men stood at their posts on the larboard side, stark naked, each armed with a grapnel, four of them on the gunwale, two of them aloft. At the moment of impact these grapnels were slung to bind the Spaniard to them, those aloft being intended to complete and preserve the entanglement of the rigging.
Aboard the rudely awakened galleon all was confused hurrying, scurrying, trumpeting, and shouting.

===Chinese===

隔着一段距离，是哈格索普统带的“伊丽莎白号”，同船的有如今没了船的卡于萨克和他的大部分法国部下。殿后的是第二条单桅船和八条独木船，装着俘虏、奴隶和大半缴获的货物。俘虏全被反绑，由四名持短铳的海盗看守——这些船除两个操帆的，就靠他们操船。他们的位置在队尾，即将来临的接战，他们一概不与。
蛋白石的曙光初融夜色时，海盗们望穿了的眼，辨出了西班牙船队高高的桅樯——就泊在前头不到四分之一英里处。西班牙人全无戒心，又仗着实力压倒，多半连平日那点懈怠的守望都懒得加强。可以断定，在那昏光里，布拉德的舰队望见他们许久之后，他们才望见布拉德的舰队。待他们真正惊动起来，伍尔夫斯通的单桅船几乎已扑到眼前——大帆船的影子一露头，它就张满了桅上的帆，直抢过来。
伍尔夫斯通驾着单桅船，直取提督的大船“恩卡纳西翁号”；随后他把舵拴死，从身边悬着的一根点好的火绳上，引燃一支浸透了沥青的粗草大炬。先是一团红光，等他抡圆了在头顶一挥，它轰地燃成烈焰，恰在此时，这轻捷的小船轰隆隆、砰砰嘭嘭擦着旗舰的船舷撞了上去，索具同索具绞作一团，头顶的桁木吱嘎作响、断杆乱迸。他的六条汉子各守左舷岗位，浑身赤裸，各执一具抓钩，四个在舷墙上，两个在桅上。撞击的一刻，这些抓钩甩出去，把西班牙船同他们锁死；桅上那两具，为的是把索具的纠缠锁个齐全结实。
被粗暴惊醒的大帆船上，一片慌乱的奔突、号角和喊叫。

===Original===

At first there had been a desperately hurried attempt to get up the anchor; but this was abandoned as being already too late; and conceiving themselves on the point of being boarded, the Spaniards stood to arms to ward off the onslaught. Its slowness in coming intrigued them, being so different from the usual tactics of the buccaneers. Further intrigued were they by the sight of the gigantic Wolverstone speeding naked along his deck with a great flaming torch held high. Not until he had completed his work did they begin to suspect the truth﻿—that he was lighting slow-matches﻿—and then one of their officers rendered reckless by panic ordered a boarding-party on to the sloop.
The order came too late. Wolverstone had seen his six fellows drop overboard after the grapnels were fixed, and then had sped, himself, to the starboard gunwale. Thence he flung his flaming torch down the nearest gaping scuttle into the hold, and thereupon dived overboard in his turn, to be picked up presently by the longboat from the *Arabella*. But before that happened the sloop was a thing of fire, from which explosions were hurling blazing combustibles aboard the *Encarnación*, and long tongues of flame were licking out to consume the galleon, beating back those daring Spaniards who, too late, strove desperately to cut her adrift.
And whilst the most formidable vessel of the Spanish fleet was thus being put out of action at the outset, Blood had sailed in to open fire upon the *Salvador*. First athwart her hawse he had loosed a broadside that had swept her decks with terrific effect, then going on and about, he had put a second broadside into her hull at short range. Leaving her thus half-crippled, temporarily, at least, and keeping to his course, he had bewildered the crew of the *Infanta* by a couple of shots from the chasers on his beak-head, then crashed alongside to grapple and board her, whilst Hagthorpe was doing the like by the *San Felipe*.
And in all this time not a single shot had the Spaniards contrived to fire, so completely had they been taken by surprise, and so swift and paralyzing had been Blood’s stroke.
Boarded now and faced by the cold steel of the buccaneers, neither the *San Felipe* nor the *Infanta* offered much resistance.

===Chinese===

起初他们还慌慌张张试着起锚；眼见已迟，只得作罢；自以为眼看要被登舷，西班牙人执兵守御，只等迎击。可这一击迟迟不来，倒叫他们犯了咟咕——海盗惯常的路数不是这样。更叫他们犯咟咕的，是那个巨人伍尔夫斯通赤条条地在甲板上飞奔、高擎一支燃着大火炬的景象。直到他把活儿做完了，他们才开始疑到真相——他在点缓燃火信——这时他们中间一名军官慌得昏了头，下令登舷反夺那条单桅船。
命令来得太迟。伍尔夫斯通眼看六个伙计在抓钩锁牢之后跳了海，自己也飞奔到右舷舷边。他从那里把燃着的火炬顺着最近的敞开舱口掷进船腹，随后自己也翻身入水，不一刻便被“阿拉贝拉号”的长艇捞起。可在那之前，单桅船已成一团火，一串爆炸把燃烧的引火物抛上“恩卡纳西翁号”，长长的火舌舔出来吞卷大帆船，逼退了那些胆敢扑上来拼命听缆的西班牙人——到底迟了。
西班牙舰队最可怕的一条船就这样开场便废了；与此同时，布拉德已驶近“萨尔瓦多号”开火。他先横过它的船头，放过一舷齐射，把它的甲板扫得七零八落；随后一路前驶、掉头，近距离又朝它的船身塞进第二舷齐射。把它这样暂且打得半瘫，他守住航向，用船头短炮朝“因凡塔号”放了两响、搅得它船员发懵，随即撞舷抓钩、登船接舷；同一时刻，哈格索普也对“圣费利佩号”如法炮制。
而这整段时间里，西班牙人一炮也没能放出来——他们被袭得这样彻底，布拉德这一击又这样迅疾、这样叫人瘫痪。
接舷既成，直面海盗的冷钢，“圣费利佩号”同“因凡塔号”都没怎么抵抗。

===Original===

The sight of their admiral in flames, and the *Salvador* drifting crippled from the action, had so utterly disheartened them that they accounted themselves vanquished, and laid down their arms.
If by a resolute stand the *Salvador* had encouraged the other two undamaged vessels to resistance, the Spaniards might well have retrieved the fortunes of the day. But it happened that the *Salvador* was handicapped in true Spanish fashion by being the treasure-ship of the fleet, with plate on board to the value of some fifty thousand pieces. Intent above all upon saving this from falling into the hands of the pirates, Don Miguel, who, with a remnant of his crew, had meanwhile transferred himself aboard her, headed her down towards Palomas and the fort that guarded the passage. This fort the Admiral, in those days of waiting, had taken the precaution secretly to garrison and rearm. For the purpose he had stripped the fort of Cojero, farther out on the gulf, of its entire armament, which included some cannon-royal of more than ordinary range and power.
With no suspicion of this, Captain Blood gave chase, accompanied by the *Infanta*, which was manned now by a prize-crew under the command of Yberville. The stern chasers of the *Salvador* desultorily returned the punishing fire of the pursuers; but such was the damage she, herself, sustained, that presently, coming under the guns of the fort, she began to sink, and finally settled down in the shallows with part of her hull above water. Thence, some in boats and some by swimming, the Admiral got his crew ashore on Palomas as best he could.
And then, just as Captain Blood accounted the victory won, and that his way out of that trap to the open sea beyond lay clear, the fort suddenly revealed its formidable and utterly unsuspected strength. With a roar the cannons-royal proclaimed themselves, and the *Arabella* staggered under a blow that smashed her bulwarks at the waist and scattered death and confusion among the seamen gathered there.
Had not Pitt, her master, himself seized the whipstaff and put the helm hard over to swing her sharply off to starboard, she must have suffered still worse from the second volley that followed fast upon the first.
Meanwhile it had fared even worse with the frailer *Infanta*.

===Chinese===

眼见提督的旗舰烈焰冲天，“萨尔瓦多号”带伤漂出战团，它们心气彻底垮了，自认败局，放下了武器。
倘若“萨尔瓦多号”咬死顶住、鼓起那两条未损船只的斗志，西班牙人本大有机会扳回这一天的命运。偏偏“萨尔瓦多号”吃了地道西班牙式的亏：它是舰队的金银船，船上载着约值五万枚八里亚尔的银器。为保这批银子不落海盗之手——重于一切——已率残部转移到它船上的堂米格尔，掉头朝帕洛马斯和守着航路的堡垒驶去。那座堡垒，提督候命的这些天里，早偷偷布了兵、添了炮。为此他把海湾更外头的科赫雷罗（Cojero）堡的军械搬了个精光，其中有几门超乎寻常射程和威力的巨型加农炮。
布拉德船长对此毫无所觉，一路追来，随行的是“因凡塔号”——如今由伊贝维尔率一批接管船员驾驶。“萨尔瓦多号”的尾炮零零落落地回击追兵的惩罚性炮火；可它自己伤得太重，行到堡垒炮口之下，便开始下沉，末了坐进浅水，半截船身还露在水面上。提督带着船员，有的上小艇、有的泗水，好歹上了帕洛马斯岸。
就在布拉德船长算计着胜局已定、出陷阱通外海的路已经敞亮的时候，堡垒忽然亮出了它那可怕得无人疑心的实力。一声轰响，巨型加农炮报了名，“阿拉贝拉号”挨了一记，船腰的舷墙砸得粉碎，聚在那里的水手死伤狼藉、乱作一团。
若不是它的船长皮特亲手抢过舵柄，把舵狠狠打向右舷、猛地荡开船头，紧跟着第一排炮弹到来的第二排，必叫它伤得更重。
与此同时，单薄的“因凡塔号”的遭际更糟。

===Original===

Although hit by one shot only, this had crushed her larboard timbers on the waterline, starting a leak that must presently have filled her, but for the prompt action of the experienced Yberville in ordering her larboard guns to be flung overboard. Thus lightened, and listing now to starboard, he fetched her about, and went staggering after the retreating *Arabella*, followed by the fire of the fort, which did them, however, little further damage.
Out of range, at last, they lay to, joined by the *Elizabeth* and the *San Felipe*, to consider their position.

===Chinese===

它虽只中了一弹，那弹却砸烂了它水线处的左舷船骨，漏起水来，不消多久便要灌满；多亏老练的伊贝维尔当机立断，下令把左舷大炮尽数抛下海。船身一轻、向右倾斜着，他掉转船头，摇摇晃晃地追着退走的“阿拉贝拉号”，堡垒的炮火一路送着他们，倒没再添多少伤。
出了射程，他们终于停下船，“伊丽莎白号”和“圣费利佩号”也来会合，商议处境。

## XVII The Dupes / 十七 受骗者
===Original===

It was a crestfallen Captain Blood who presided over that hastily summoned council held on the poop-deck of the *Arabella* in the brilliant morning sunshine. It was, he declared afterwards, one of the bitterest moments in his career. He was compelled to digest the fact that having conducted the engagement with a skill of which he might justly be proud, having destroyed a force so superior in ships and guns and men that Don Miguel de Espinosa had justifiably deemed it overwhelming, his victory was rendered barren by three lucky shots from an unsuspected battery by which they had been surprised. And barren must their victory remain until they could reduce the fort that still remained to defend the passage.
At first Captain Blood was for putting his ships in order and making the attempt there and then. But the others dissuaded him from betraying an impetuosity usually foreign to him, and born entirely of chagrin and mortification, emotions which will render unreasonable the most reasonable of men. With returning calm, he surveyed the situation. The *Arabella* was no longer in case to put to sea; the *Infanta* was merely kept afloat by artifice, and the *San Felipe* was almost as sorely damaged by the fire she had sustained from the buccaneers before surrendering.
Clearly, then, he was compelled to admit in the end that nothing remained but to return to Maracaybo, there to refit the ships before attempting to force the passage.
And so, back to Maracaybo came those defeated victors of that short, terrible fight. And if anything had been wanting further to exasperate their leader, he had it in the pessimism of which Cahusac did not economize expressions. Transported at first to heights of dizzy satisfaction by the swift and easy victory of their inferior force that morning, the Frenchman was now plunged back and more deeply than ever into the abyss of hopelessness. And his mood infected at least the main body of his own followers.
“It is the end,” he told Captain Blood. “This time we are checkmated.”
“I’ll take the liberty of reminding you that you said the same before,” Captain Blood answered him as patiently as he could. “Yet you’ve seen what you’ve seen, and you’ll not deny that in ships and guns we are returning stronger than we went. Look at our present fleet, man.”
“I am looking at it,” said Cahusac.
“Pish! Ye’re a white-livered cur when all is said.”

===Chinese===

那天早晨阳光明晃晃，“阿拉贝拉号”艉楼上匆匆召集的军事会议由布拉德船长主持——垂头丧气的布拉德船长。他后来宣称，那是他生涯里最苦的时刻之一。他不得不咽下这样一个事实：这一仗他打得那样的漂亮——漂亮得足以自豪——歼灭了一支在船、炮、人上都占压倒优势、令堂米格尔·德·埃斯皮诺萨有充分理由视之为雷霆的兵力，而他的胜利，却被一座意想不到的炮台的三发走运炮弹断送成了空欢喜。而且这胜利还得空下去，直到他们拿下那座仍在扼守航道的要塞。
起初布拉德船长主张把船整一整，当场就干。可旁人劝住了他，劝他别露出那股素来不属于他的急躁——那急躁纯出于懊恼和羞愤，而这两种情绪，最能叫通情达理的人也变得不讲理。冷静回来后，他估量了局面。“阿拉贝拉号”一时出不了海；“因凡塔号”（*Infanta*）全凭手段吊着不沉；“圣费利佩号”（*San Felipe*）在投降前换了海盗们那顿炮火，也伤得不轻。
于是，到头来他只得承认：别无他法，唯有退回马拉开波，把船修整好了再图强渡水道。
就这样，那场短促而可怕的战斗里打胜了的败军之将们，回到了马拉开波。倘若还差什么把他们的头领再激一激，卡于萨克那毫不节省言辞的悲观主义尽够了。那天早晨己方劣势兵力干脆利落的胜利，先把他抛上了晕眩的满足之巅，此刻他又跌回无望的深渊，跌得比以往更深。他的情绪至少感染了他自己部下的大多数。
“完了，”他对布拉德船长说，“这回咱们是将死了。”
“恕我冒昧提醒一句：这话你从前也说过。”布拉德船长尽量耐心地答他。“可后来你亲眼见了什么。你总不能否认，在船上、在炮上，我们回去时比来时更强。瞧瞧咱们眼下的舰队，伙计。”
“我正瞧着呢。”卡于萨克说。
“呸！说到底，你就是条白肚皮的狗。”

===Original===

“You call me a coward?”
“I’ll take that liberty.”
The Breton glared at him, breathing hard. But he had no mind to ask satisfaction for the insult. He knew too well the kind of satisfaction that Captain Blood was likely to afford him. He remembered the fate of Levasseur. So he confined himself to words.
“It is too much! You go too far!” he complained bitterly.
“Look you, Cahusac: it’s sick and tired I am of your perpetual whining and complaining when things are not as smooth as a convent dining-table. If ye wanted things smooth and easy, ye shouldn’t have taken to the sea, and ye should never ha’ sailed with me, for with me things are never smooth and easy. And that, I think, is all I have to say to you this morning.”
Cahusac flung away cursing, and went to take the feeling of his men.
Captain Blood went off to give his surgeon’s skill to the wounded, among whom he remained engaged until late afternoon. Then, at last, he went ashore, his mind made up, and returned to the house of the Governor, to indite a truculent but very scholarly letter in purest Castilian to Don Miguel.
“I have shown your excellency this morning of what I am capable,” he wrote. “Although outnumbered by more than two to one in men, in ships, and in guns, I have sunk or captured the vessels of the great fleet with which you were to come to Maracaybo to destroy us. So that you are no longer in case to carry out your boast, even when your reinforcements on the *Santo Niño*, reach you from La Guayra. From what has occurred, you may judge of what must occur. I should not trouble your excellency with this letter but that I am a humane man, abhorring bloodshed. Therefore before proceeding to deal with your fort, which you may deem invincible, as I have dealt already with your fleet, which you deemed invincible, I make you, purely out of humanitarian considerations, this last offer of terms. I will spare this city of Maracaybo and forthwith evacuate it, leaving behind me the forty prisoners I have taken, in consideration of your paying me the sum of fifty thousand pieces of eight and one hundred head of cattle as a ransom, thereafter granting me unmolested passage of the bar. My prisoners, most of whom are persons of consideration, I will retain as hostages until after my departure, sending them back in the canoes which we shall take with us for that purpose.

===Chinese===

“你骂我懦夫？”
“我就冒这个昧。”
布列塔尼人瞪着他，喘着粗气。可他无心为这句侮辱讨什么公道。布拉德船长会给他什么样的公道，他清楚得很。勒瓦瑟的下场他记得。于是他只动嘴。
“欺人太甚！你太过分了！”他愤愤地抱怨。
“你听着，卡于萨克：事情一不顺——顺得不像修道院的餐桌——你就哼哼唧唧、怨天怨地，我早听腻了、听烦了。你要的是事事平顺舒坦，就不该下海，更不该随我出海，因为跟着我，事情从来没有平顺舒坦过。我想，今天早上我要对你说的，就这些了。”
卡于萨克骂骂咧咧地走了，去摸他那些人的口气。
布拉德船长去把他的外科本事用在伤员身上，一直忙到下午很晚。然后他终于上了岸，主意已定，回到总督的宅子，用最纯正的卡斯蒂利亚文，给堂米格尔炮制了一封咄咄逼人却极有学问的信。
“今天早晨，我已向阁下展示过我的手段，”他写道，“贵军在人数、船只、炮火上均倍于我方，我仍将阁下开来马拉开波、要消灭我们的那支大舰队的船只，或击沉，或俘获。是以阁下已无力兑现您的豪言，即便您在拉瓜伊拉上船、开到‘圣尼诺号’（*Santo Niño*）上的援兵到齐也是枉然。观诸既往，可卜将来。本不欲以此信烦扰阁下，只因我是个讲人道的人，憎恶流血。故在处置阁下的要塞之前——这座要塞您也许视为不可攻克，正如您的舰队，您也曾视为不可攻克，而我已处置过了——我纯粹出于人道主义的考虑，向您提出最后的条件。如阁下付我赎金五万块八里亚尔、牛一百头，并许我无扰渡过拦门沙，我便饶下马拉开波这座城，即刻撤离，撇下我俘获的四十名俘虏。我这些俘虏多半是有身份的人，我将扣作人质，直到我离开之后，再用我们为此带走的独木舟把他们送回。

===Original===

If your excellency should be so ill-advised as to refuse these terms, and thereby impose upon me the necessity of reducing your fort at the cost of some lives, I warn you that you may expect no quarter from us, and that I shall begin by leaving a heap of ashes where this pleasant city of Maracaybo now stands.”
The letter written, he bade them bring him from among the prisoners the Deputy-Governor of Maracaybo, who had been taken at Gibraltar. Disclosing its contents to him, he despatched him with it to Don Miguel.
His choice of a messenger was shrewd. The Deputy-Governor was of all men the most anxious for the deliverance of his city, the one man who on his own account would plead most fervently for its preservation at all costs from the fate with which Captain Blood was threatening it. And as he reckoned so it befell. The Deputy-Governor added his own passionate pleading to the proposals of the letter.
But Don Miguel was of stouter heart. True, his fleet had been partly destroyed and partly captured. But then, he argued, he had been taken utterly by surprise. That should not happen again. There should be no surprising the fort. Let Captain Blood do his worst at Maracaybo, there should be a bitter reckoning for him when eventually he decided﻿—as, sooner or later, decide he must﻿—to come forth. The Deputy-Governor was flung into panic. He lost his temper, and said some hard things to the Admiral. But they were not as hard as the thing the Admiral said to him in answer.
“Had you been as loyal to your King in hindering the entrance of these cursed pirates as I shall be in hindering their going forth again, we should not now find ourselves in our present straits. So weary me no more with your coward counsels. I make no terms with Captain Blood. I know my duty to my King, and I intend to perform it. I also know my duty to myself. I have a private score with this rascal, and I intend to settle it. Take you that message back.”
So back to Maracaybo, back to his own handsome house in which Captain Blood had established his quarters, came the Deputy-Governor with the Admiral’s answer. And because he had been shamed into a show of spirit by the Admiral’s own stout courage in adversity, he delivered it as truculently as the Admiral could have desired.
“And is it like that?”

===Chinese===

“阁下若不智到拒受这些条件、逼我在付出若干性命的代价后拿下您的要塞，我警告您：休想从我这里得到饶恕；而我的头一件事，就是叫这座宜人的马拉开波城，变成一堆灰烬。”
信写好，他吩咐从在直布罗陀俘获的俘虏里提出马拉开波的副总督，把信念给他听，派他把信送去给堂米格尔。
这信使挑得精明。副总督是全城最急于解围的人，是唯一一个为了自己也必定会苦苦哀求、不惜一切代价保住此城免遭布拉德船长威胁的命运的人。果如所料，副总督把自己激烈的哀求，加到了信的条件上。
可堂米格尔的心肠更硬。不错，他的舰队一半被毁一半被俘。可是，他辩道，那是猝不及防。这种事不会再有。要塞是猝袭不了的。随布拉德船长在马拉开波怎样撒野，等他终于决定出关时——这一步他早晚必须走——自有苦账同他算。副总督慌了，发了脾气，对上将说了些难听的话。可他那些话，抵不上上将回敬他的那一句难听。
“你当初若能像我誓死堵住这些该死海盗的退路那样，尽忠你的国王、堵住他们的进路，我们今天何至于落到这般田地。所以，别再拿你那些懦夫的主张来烦我。我不同布拉德船长讲条件。我懂得我对国王的本分，我打算尽到它。我也懂得我对我自己的本分。我同这恶棍有一笔私账，我打算清了它。把这句话带回去。”
副总督带着上将的答复回了马拉开波，回到布拉德船长安了营的那座漂亮宅子——他自己的宅子。因为被上将在逆境中的硬气臊出了一点血性，他把这答复转述得咄咄逼人，正合上将的心意。
“哦，是这样？”

===Original===

said Captain Blood with a quiet smile, though the heart of him sank at this failure of his bluster. “Well, well, it’s a pity now that the Admiral’s so headstrong. It was that way he lost his fleet, which was his own to lose. This pleasant city of Maracaybo isn’t. So no doubt he’ll lose it with fewer misgivings. I am sorry. Waste, like bloodshed, is a thing abhorrent to me. But there ye are! I’ll have the faggots to the place in the morning, and maybe when he sees the blaze tomorrow night he’ll begin to believe that Peter Blood is a man of his word. Ye may go, Don Francisco.”
The Deputy-Governor went out with dragging feet, followed by guards, his momentary truculence utterly spent.
But no sooner had he departed than up leapt Cahusac, who had been of the council assembled to receive the Admiral’s answer. His face was white and his hands shook as he held them out in protest.
“Death of my life, what have you to say now?” he cried, his voice husky. And without waiting to hear what it might be, he raved on: “I knew you not frighten the Admiral so easy. He hold us entrapped, and he knows it; yet you dream that he will yield himself to your impudent message. Your fool letter it have seal’ the doom of us all.”
“Have ye done?” quoth Blood quietly, as the Frenchman paused for breath.
“No, I have not.”
“Then spare me the rest. It’ll be of the same quality, devil a doubt, and it doesn’t help us to solve the riddle that’s before us.”
“But what are you going to do? Is it that you will tell me?” It was not a question, it was a demand.
“How the devil do I know? I was hoping you’d have some ideas yourself. But since ye’re so desperately concerned to save your skin, you and those that think like you are welcome to leave us. I’ve no doubt at all the Spanish Admiral will welcome the abatement of our numbers even at this late date. Ye shall have the sloop as a parting gift from us, and ye can join Don Miguel in the fort for all I care, or for all the good ye’re likely to be to us in this present pass.”
“It is to my men to decide,” Cahusac retorted, swallowing his fury, and on that stalked out to talk to them, leaving the others to deliberate in peace.
Next morning early he sought Captain Blood again. He found him alone in the patio, pacing to and fro, his head sunk on his breast. Cahusac mistook consideration for dejection.

===Chinese===

布拉德船长说，带着安静的微笑，尽管虚张声势落了空，他的心沉了下去。“唉，唉，上将这么倔，真可惜。他的舰队就是这么丢的——那舰队是他自己的，丢了活该。这座宜人的马拉开波城可不是他的。所以他丢起来，无疑会少些顾忌。我很遗憾。浪费，同流血一样，是我憎恶的东西。可事已如此！明早我就叫人往城里码柴捆，兴许明晚他望见火光，就会开始相信彼得·布拉德是说话算话的人了。您去吧，堂弗朗西斯科。”
副总督拖着脚出去了，看守跟着，他那片刻的凶横已荡然无存。
可他前脚一走，卡于萨克后脚就跳了起来——他也列席了听取上将答复的会议。他脸白手颤，把双手伸出来抗议。
“要我的命，这下你还有什么说的？”他哑着嗓子喊。不等听对方有什么说的，他自顾自骂下去：“我早知道你吓不倒上将。他把咱们困死了，他自己清楚；你倒做梦他会顺了你那封无耻的信。你那封蠢信，把咱们大伙全判了。”
“说完了吗？”等那法国人换气，布拉德静静地问。
“没有。”
“那就省下后半截。准是一个味儿，没错，可它帮不上咱们解眼前这个谜。”
“那你打算怎么办？肯不肯告诉我？”这不是问句，是逼供。
“我鬼知道怎么办？我还指望你们自己出点主意呢。可你既这样豁出命去保你那张皮，你，还有同你想得一模一样的人，请便，离开我们。西班牙上将一定欢迎咱们的人手就此减一截，哪怕这么晚。那条单桅船给你们，算我们临别的礼物；你们去要塞里投堂米格尔也要得——我管不着——眼下这个关口，你们留在我们这儿，多半也是废物。”
“轮不到我，得我的人说了算。”卡于萨克咽下怒火回敬，说罢大步出去找他的人，留下其余的人清静议事。
第二天一早他又来找布拉德船长，见他独自在天井里踱步，头垂在胸前。卡于萨克把沉思错认成了沮丧。

===Original===

Each of us carries in himself a standard by which to measure his neighbour.
“We have take’ you at your word, Captain,” he announced, between sullenness and defiance. Captain Blood paused, shoulders hunched, hands behind his back, and mildly regarded the buccaneer in silence. Cahusac explained himself. “Last night I send one of my men to the Spanish Admiral with a letter. I make him offer to capitulate if he will accord us passage with the honours of war. This morning I receive his answer. He accord us this on the understanding that we carry nothing away with us. My men they are embarking them on the sloop. We sail at once.”
“Bon voyage,” said Captain Blood, and with a nod he turned on his heel again to resume his interrupted mediation.
“Is that all that you have to say to me?” cried Cahusac.
“There are other things,” said Blood over his shoulder. “But I know ye wouldn’t like them.”
“Ha! Then it’s adieu, my Captain.” Venomously he added: “It is my belief that we shall not meet again.”
“Your belief is my hope,” said Captain Blood.
Cahusac flung away, obscenely vituperative. Before noon he was under way with his followers, some sixty dejected men who had allowed themselves to be persuaded by him into that empty-handed departure﻿—in spite even of all that Yberville could do to prevent it. The Admiral kept faith with him, and allowed him free passage out to sea, which, from his knowledge of Spaniards, was more than Captain Blood had expected.
Meanwhile, no sooner had the deserters weighed anchor than Captain Blood received word that the Deputy-Governor begged to be allowed to see him again. Admitted, Don Francisco at once displayed the fact that a night’s reflection had quickened his apprehensions for the city of Maracaybo and his condemnation of the Admiral’s intransigence.
Captain Blood received him pleasantly.
“Good morning to you, Don Francisco. I have postponed the bonfire until nightfall. It will make a better show in the dark.”
Don Francisco, a slight, nervous, elderly man of high lineage and low vitality, came straight to business.
“I am here to tell you, Don Pedro, that if you will hold your hand for three days, I will undertake to raise the ransom you demand, which Don Miguel de Espinosa refuses.”
Captain Blood confronted him, a frown contracting the dark brows above his light eyes:
“And where will you be raising it?”

===Chinese===

我们每个人身上，都随身带着一把量邻居的尺。
“我们照你的话办了，船长。”他半是阴沉半是挑衅地宣布。布拉德船长顿住，耸着肩，手背在身后，默默地和气打量这海盗。卡于萨克说明来意。“昨夜我派了我一个人去见西班牙上将，带了封信。我向他提议：若他准我们凭军礼通过，我们便投降。今早我得了回音。他准了，条件是我们什么也不许带走。我的人正在往单桅船上搬。我们即刻开船。”
“一路顺风。”布拉德船长说，点点头，转身又踱起他被打断的方步。
“你要对我说的就这一句？”卡于萨克喊。
“还有几句，”布拉德隔着肩膀说，“可我知道你不会爱听。”
“哈！那就永别了，我的船长。”他恶毒地补一句：“我信，咱们不会再见面了。”
“你的信念就是我的希望。”布拉德船长说。
卡于萨克骂骂咧咧地去了。不到中午，他带着他的追随者起了锚——约莫六十个垂头丧气的人，被他劝得答应了两手空空地走——尽管伊贝维尔（Yberville）百般阻拦也无济于事。上将对他守了信，放他们自由出港；凭他对西班牙人的了解，这已经超出布拉德船长的期望了。
其间，逃兵们前脚起锚，后脚就有信到：副总督求见。获准进来后，堂弗朗西斯科立刻表明：一夜的思量，加深了他对马拉开波城的忧虑，也加深了他对上将死硬的谴责。
布拉德船长客气地接待他。
“早上好，堂弗朗西斯科。我把篝火推迟到了天黑。黑里烧起来更好看。”
堂弗朗西斯科——一个瘦小、神经质、出身高贵而元气低微的老者——开门见山。
“我来告诉您，堂佩德罗：您若肯住手三天，我担保筹齐您要的赎金——堂米格尔·德·埃斯皮诺萨不肯付的那笔。”
布拉德船长面对着他，浅色眼睛上方那两道黑眉蹙起：
“那您上哪儿筹去？”

===Original===

quoth he, faintly betraying his surprise.
Don Francisco shook his head. “That must remain my affair,” he answered. “I know where it is to be found, and my compatriots must contribute. Give me leave for three days on parole, and I will see you fully satisfied. Meanwhile my son remains in your hands as a hostage for my return.” And upon that he fell to pleading. But in this he was crisply interrupted.
“By the Saints! Ye’re a bold man, Don Francisco, to come to me with such a tale﻿—to tell me that ye know where the ransom’s to be raised, and yet to refuse to say. D’ye think now that with a match between your fingers ye’d grow more communicative?”
If Don Francisco grew a shade paler, yet again he shook his head.
“That was the way of Morgan and L’Ollonais and other pirates. But it is not the way of Captain Blood. If I had doubted that I should not have disclosed so much.”
The Captain laughed. “You old rogue,” said he. “Ye play upon my vanity, do you?”
“Upon your honour, Captain.”
“The honour of a pirate? Ye’re surely crazed!”
“The honour of Captain Blood,” Don Francisco insisted. “You have the repute of making war like a gentleman.”
Captain Blood laughed again, on a bitter, sneering note that made Don Francisco fear the worst. He was not to guess that it was himself the Captain mocked.
“That’s merely because it’s more remunerative in the end. And that is why you are accorded the three days you ask for. So about it, Don Francisco. You shall have what mules you need. I’ll see to it.”
Away went Don Francisco on his errand, leaving Captain Blood to reflect, between bitterness and satisfaction, that a reputation for as much chivalry as is consistent with piracy is not without its uses.
Punctually on the third day the Deputy-Governor was back in Maracaybo with his mules laden with plate and money to the value demanded and a herd of a hundred head of cattle driven in by negro slaves.
These bullocks were handed over to those of the company who ordinarily were boucan-hunters, and therefore skilled in the curing of meats, and for best part of a week thereafter they were busy at the waterside with the quartering and salting of carcases.
While this was doing on the one hand and the ships were being refitted for sea on the other, Captain Blood was pondering the riddle on the solution of which his own fate depended.

===Chinese===

他问，微露出一点意外。
堂弗朗西斯科摇头。“那得还是我的私事。”他答。“我知道哪里筹得着，我的同胞们也必须出力。凭誓言放我三天假，我保您分文不差。此间，我儿子留在您手里，作我回来的人质。”说完他开始哀求。可这回他被干脆地打断了。
“凭圣徒起誓！您真是条汉子，堂弗朗西斯科，敢带着这么一套说辞来见我——告诉我您知道赎金哪里筹，却又死不肯说。您想想，倘若您指头间夹根点着的火绳，您话会不会多一些？”
堂弗朗西斯科纵然又白了一层，还是摇头。
“那是摩根、罗罗内和别的海盗的做法。可不是布拉德船长的做法。我若疑心这个，方才就不会吐那么多。”
船长笑了。“你这老滑头，”他说，“你弹我的虚荣心，是不是？”
“弹您的名誉，船长。”
“海盗的名誉？你准是疯了！”
“布拉德船长的名誉。”堂弗朗西斯科坚持。“您有打仗像绅士的名声。”
布拉德船长又笑了，笑得刻毒，笑得讥诮，吓得堂弗朗西斯科以为要糟。他猜不到，船长嘲弄的是他自己。
“那不过是因为这样到头来更合算。所以你要的三天，就批给你。去办吧，堂弗朗西斯科。你需要多少骡子，都有。我来交代。”
堂弗朗西斯科办事去了，撇下布拉德船长独自回味，半是苦涩半是得意：一副“同海盗身份相称的、顶有骑士风度”的名声，倒也不是全无用处。
第三天，副总督分毫不差地回到马拉开波，骡子驮着足值的银器银钱，一百头牛由黑奴赶着也到了。
这些牛交给了队伍里那些原本猎牛出身的弟兄——腌肉的好手——此后大半个星期，他们都在水边忙着宰牲、剔骨、撒盐。
一边忙这个，一边船在整修出海，布拉德船长则琢磨着那道谜题——他自己的命运，全系在这道题的解上。

===Original===

Indian spies whom he employed brought him word that the Spaniards, working at low tide, had salved the thirty guns of the *Salvador*, and thus had added yet another battery to their already overwhelming strength. In the end, and hoping for inspiration on the spot, Captain Blood made a reconnaissance in person. At the risk of his life, accompanied by two friendly Indians, he crossed to the island in a canoe under cover of dark. They concealed themselves and the canoe in the short thick scrub with which that side of the island was densely covered, and lay there until daybreak. Then Blood went forward alone, and with infinite precaution, to make his survey. He went to verify a suspicion that he had formed, and approached the fort as nearly as he dared and a deal nearer than was safe.
On all fours he crawled to the summit of an eminence a mile or so away, whence he found himself commanding a view of the interior dispositions of the stronghold. By the aid of a telescope with which he had equipped himself he was able to verify that, as he had suspected and hoped, the fort’s artillery was all mounted on the seaward side.
Satisfied, he returned to Maracaybo, and laid before the six who composed his council﻿—Pitt, Hagthorpe, Yberville, Wolverstone, Dyke, and Ogle﻿—a proposal to storm the fort from the landward side. Crossing to the island under cover of night, they would take the Spaniards by surprise and attempt to overpower them before they could shift their guns to meet the onslaught.
With the exception of Wolverstone, who was by temperament the kind of man who favours desperate chances, those officers received the proposal coldly. Hagthorpe incontinently opposed it.
“It’s a harebrained scheme, Peter,” he said gravely, shaking his handsome head. “Consider now that we cannot depend upon approaching unperceived to a distance whence we might storm the fort before the cannon could be moved. But even if we could, we can take no cannon ourselves; we must depend entirely upon our small arms, and how shall we, a bare three hundred” (for this was the number to which Cahusac’s defection had reduced them), “cross the open to attack more than twice that number under cover?”
The others﻿—Dyke, Ogle, Yberville, and even Pitt, whom loyalty to Blood may have made reluctant﻿—loudly approved him. When they had done, “I have considered all,” said Captain Blood.

===Chinese===

他雇的印第安探子来报：西班牙人趁着低潮，把“萨尔瓦多号”（*Salvador*）上的三十门炮打捞了上来，又给他们本已压倒的火力添上了一座炮台。末了，指望现场得些灵感，布拉德船长亲自去踏勘了一回。他冒着一生的风险，由两个交好的印第安人陪着，趁黑驾独木舟渡到岛上，把人和舟藏进岛那边密密的矮树丛，趴到天亮。然后布拉德独自上前，怀着无限的谨慎去做他的勘察。他去验证心里早已成形的一个疑心，尽可能近地逼近要塞——近过了他敢、也近过了安全的界线。
他手脚并用地爬上一座一英里外的高地，发觉自己正俯瞰要塞内部的部署。借着一架备好的望远镜，他得以证实：正如他所疑、所盼，要塞的火炮，一门不剩，全装在了临海那面。
放了心，他回到马拉开波，把一个方案摆给他那六人组成的军事会议——皮特、哈格索普、伊贝维尔、伍尔夫斯通、戴克、奥格尔：从陆那面强攻要塞。趁夜渡到岛上，打西班牙人一个猝不及防，赶在他们挪炮迎击之前压垮他们。
除了生性偏爱亡命一搏的伍尔夫斯通，军官们对这方案反应冷淡。哈格索普当场反对。
“这是个异想天开的计划，彼得。”他摇着英俊的脑袋，郑重地说。“想想看：我们指望不上不被察觉地接近到能抢下要塞的距离——赶在炮挪动之前。可就算能，我们自己又带不了炮；全得指望手里的轻火器；而我们，区区三百人”（卡于萨克的叛逃把他们减到了这个数）“凭什么穿过开阔地，去攻两倍于我们的、有掩体的人？”
其余的人——戴克、奥格尔、伊贝维尔，甚至忠于布拉德而一度迟疑的皮特——都大声附和他。等他们说完了：“这一切我都掂量过了。”布拉德船长说。

===Original===

“I have weighed the risks and studied how to lessen them. In these desperate straits﻿ ﻿…”
He broke off abruptly. A moment he frowned, deep in thought; then his face was suddenly alight with inspiration. Slowly he drooped his head, and sat there considering, weighing, chin on breast. Then he nodded, muttering, “Yes,” and again, “Yes.” He looked up, to face them. “Listen,” he cried. “You may be right. The risks may be too heavy. Whether or not, I have thought of a better way. That which should have been the real attack shall be no more than a feint. Here, then, is the plan I now propose.”
He talked swiftly and clearly, and as he talked one by one his officers’ faces became alight with eagerness. When he had done, they cried as with one voice that he had saved them.
“That is yet to be proved in action,” said he.
Since for the last twenty-four hours all had been in readiness for departure, there was nothing now to delay them, and it was decided to move next morning.
Such was Captain Blood’s assurance of success that he immediately freed the prisoners held as hostages, and even the negro slaves, who were regarded by the others as legitimate plunder. His only precaution against those released prisoners was to order them into the church and there lock them up, to await deliverance at the hands of those who should presently be coming into the city.
Then, all being aboard the three ships, with the treasure safely stowed in their holds and the slaves under hatches, the buccaneers weighed anchor and stood out for the bar, each vessel towing three piraguas astern.
The Admiral, beholding their stately advance in the full light of noon, their sails gleaming white in the glare of the sunlight, rubbed his long, lean hands in satisfaction, and laughed through his teeth.
“At last!” he cried. “God delivers him into my hands!” He turned to the group of staring officers behind him. “Sooner or later it had to be,” he said. “Say now, gentlemen, whether I am justified of my patience. Here end today the troubles caused to the subjects of the Catholic King by this infamous Don Pedro Sangre, as he once called himself to me.”
He turned to issue orders, and the fort became lively as a hive. The guns were manned, the gunners already kindling fuses, when the buccaneer fleet, whilst still heading for Palomas, was observed to bear away to the west. The Spaniards watched them, intrigued.

===Chinese===

“风险我称过了，也研究了怎么减。在这般绝地里……”
他猛地顿住。片刻间他皱眉苦思；随后他的脸忽然被灵感照亮。他缓缓垂下头，坐在那里掂量、权衡，下巴抵着胸口。然后他点头，喃喃道：“是。”又一声：“是。”他抬起头，面对他们。“听着，”他喊，“你们或许是对的。风险兴许太重。管它呢，我想出了一条更好的路。本当真正进攻的那一路，就只做个佯动。那么，我现在的计划是这样。”
他讲得又快又清楚，讲着讲着，军官们的脸一张接一张亮起了热切。讲完，他们齐声喊：他救了他们。
“这还得靠实战来证明。”他说。
既然过去二十四小时里一切早已整备停当，再没有什么可耽搁的，便定于次日早晨行动。
布拉德船长对成功那样有把握，以致立刻放了扣作人质的俘虏，连黑奴也放了——在旁人眼里那些本是合法的赃物。他对这批获释俘虏唯一的防备，是把他们赶进教堂锁起来，等着即将进城的人来解救。
随后，一切人等都上了三条船，财宝稳稳入了舱，奴隶押下了底舱，海盗们起锚，朝拦门沙开去，每条船后面拖着三条独木舟。
上将在正午的满光里望见他们堂堂的推进——船帆在烈日下白得耀眼——搓着那双干瘦的长手，满意得从牙缝里笑出声来。
“终于！”他喊。“上帝把他送进我手里了！”他转身对着身后那群瞪眼军官。“迟早必有这一天。”他说。“诸位评评，我的忍耐可算有了报偿。今天，这个对我自报过堂佩德罗·桑格雷的无耻之徒给天主教陛下的臣民添的麻烦，就此了结。”
他转身下令，要塞顿时活跃得像一窝蜂。各炮就位，炮手已点燃引信——就在这时，海盗舰队本朝着帕洛马斯（Palomas）开，却被看见一齐转舵向西。西班牙人纳闷地望着。

===Original===

Within a mile and a half to westward of the fort, and within a half-mile of the shore﻿—that is to say, on the very edge of the shoal water that makes Palomas unapproachable on either side by any but vessels of the shallowest draught﻿—the four ships cast anchor well within the Spaniards’ view, but just out of range of their heaviest cannon.
Sneeringly the Admiral laughed.
“Aha! They hesitate, these English dogs! *Por Dios*, and well they may.”
“They will be waiting for night,” suggested his nephew, who stood at his elbow quivering with excitement.
Don Miguel looked at him, smiling. “And what shall the night avail them in this narrow passage, under the very muzzles of my guns? Be sure, Esteban, that tonight your father will be paid for.”
He raised his telescope to continue his observation of the buccaneers. He saw that the piraguas towed by each vessel were being warped alongside, and he wondered a little what this manoeuver might portend. Awhile those piraguas were hidden from view behind the hulls. Then one by one they reappeared, rowing round and away from the ships, and each boat, he observed, was crowded with armed men. Thus laden, they were headed for the shore, at a point where it was densely wooded to the water’s edge. The eyes of the wondering Admiral followed them until the foliage screened them from his view.
Then he lowered his telescope and looked at his officers.
“What the devil does it mean?” he asked.
None answered him, all being as puzzled as he was himself.
After a little while, Esteban, who kept his eyes on the water, plucked at his uncle’s sleeve. “There they go!” he cried, and pointed.
And there, indeed, went the piraguas on their way back to the ships. But now it was observed that they were empty, save for the men who rowed them. Their armed cargo had been left ashore.
Back to the ships they pulled, to return again presently with a fresh load of armed men, which similarly they conveyed to Palomas. And at last one of the Spanish officers ventured an explanation:
“They are going to attack us by land﻿—to attempt to storm the fort.”
“Of course.” The Admiral smiled. “I had guessed it. Whom the gods would destroy they first make mad.”
“Shall we make a sally?” urged Esteban, in his excitement.
“A sally? Through that scrub? That would be to play into their hands. No, no, we will wait here to receive this attack.

===Chinese===

要塞以西一英里半、离岸半英里——也就是说，恰在使帕洛马斯两侧除最浅吃水的船外一概无法接近的浅水边缘上——四条船在西班牙人眼皮底下、却刚好在其最重火炮射程之外下了锚。
上将嗤笑。
“啊哈！他们犹豫了，这些英国狗！*Por Dios*（对着上帝），他们该犹豫。”
“他们多半在等夜里。”站在他肘边、激动得发抖的侄子提议。
堂米格尔含笑看他。“在这条窄道里，就在我的炮口底下，夜能给他们什么用？放心，埃斯特万，今夜必叫你父亲的那笔账讨还。”
他举起望远镜继续观察海盗。他看见各船拖着的独木舟正被绞到船舷旁，心里有点犯嘀咕，猜这动作预兆什么。有一阵，那些独木舟隐在船身后面看不见。随后它们一条接一条重新露面，绕开大船划出去，他观察到，每条船都挤满了武装的人。满载着，它们朝岸上一点开去——那一段岸，林木密到水边。诧异的上将眼睛跟着它们，直到树叶遮没了它们。
他放下望远镜，看他的军官们。
“这究竟是什么意思？”他问。
无人作答，个个同他自己一样纳闷。
过了一会儿，眼睛盯着水面的埃斯特万扯扯叔叔的衣袖。“它们又去了！”他喊，一指。
果然，独木舟正往大船回。可这时看得分明：船上空了，只剩划船的人。他们武装的货，都卸在了岸上。
它们划回大船，不多时又载来新的一船武装汉子，照样送到帕洛马斯。终于有一名西班牙军官大着胆子作了解释：
“他们要从陆上攻我们——企图强攻要塞。”
“当然。”上将微笑。“我早猜着了。上帝要毁灭谁，先叫他发疯。”
“咱们出击吗？”埃斯特万激切地撺掇。
“出击？穿过那片树丛？那正中他们下怀。不，不，我们就在这儿候着这一攻。

===Original===

Whenever it comes, it is themselves will be destroyed, and utterly. Have no doubt of that.”
But by evening the Admiral’s equanimity was not quite so perfect. By then the piraguas had made a half-dozen journeys with their loads of men, and they had landed also﻿—as Don Miguel had clearly observed through his telescope﻿—at least a dozen guns.
His countenance no longer smiled; it was a little wrathful and a little troubled now as he turned again to his officers.
“Who was the fool who told me that they number but three hundred men in all? They have put at least twice that number ashore already.”
Amazed as he was, his amazement would have been deeper had he been told the truth: that there was not a single buccaneer or a single gun ashore on Palomas. The deception had been complete. Don Miguel could not guess that the men he had beheld in those piraguas were always the same; that on the journeys to the shore they sat and stood upright in full view; and that on the journeys back to the ships, they lay invisible at the bottom of the boats, which were thus made to appear empty.
The growing fears of the Spanish soldiery at the prospect of a night attack from the landward side by the entire buccaneer force﻿—and a force twice as strong as they had suspected the pestilent Blood to command﻿—began to be communicated to the Admiral.
In the last hours of fading daylight, the Spaniards did precisely what Captain Blood so confidently counted that they would do﻿—precisely what they must do to meet the attack, preparations for which had been so thoroughly simulated. They set themselves to labour like the damned at those ponderous guns emplaced to command the narrow passage out to sea.
Groaning and sweating, urged on by the curses and even the whips of their officers, they toiled in a frenzy of panic-stricken haste to shift the greater number and the more powerful of their guns across to the landward side, there to emplace them anew, so that they might be ready to receive the attack which at any moment now might burst upon them from the woods not half a mile away.
Thus, when night fell, although in mortal anxiety of the onslaught of those wild devils whose reckless courage was a byword on the seas of the Main, at least the Spaniards were tolerably prepared for it. Waiting, they stood to their guns.

===Chinese===

它几时来，毁灭的、彻底毁灭的，就是他们自己。对此不必怀疑。”
可到了傍晚，上将的镇定就不那么十全了。那时独木舟已载人跑了六七个来回，而且——堂米格尔从望远镜里看得清楚——至少卸下了十二门炮。
他的脸不再笑了；再转向军官们时，有点恼，也有点忧了。
“哪个蠢才告诉我，他们统共不过三百人？他们送上岸的，已经至少两倍于此。”
他惊，可若有人告诉他真相，他还要更惊：帕洛马斯岸上，没有一个海盗、一门炮。骗局天衣无缝。堂米格尔猜不到，他在那些独木舟里望见的人，回回是同一批：去岸上时，他们坐着站着，袒露在满眼望里；回大船时，他们躺进船底，人影不见，船便显得空了。
西班牙兵眼看海盗全军——一支比他们疑心那讨厌的布拉德所统率的还强一倍的队伍——要从陆上夜袭，日渐增长的恐惧，开始传到上将身上。
白昼将尽的最后几个钟头里，西班牙人干的，恰恰是布拉德船长满有把握算定他们会干的事——恰恰是他们为迎击那场被彻头彻尾假演出来的进攻所必得干的事。他们像遭了天罚似的苦干起来，搬动那些镇守出海窄道的笨重火炮。
呻吟着，流着汗，被军官的咒骂乃至鞭子驱赶着，他们亡命地、慌不择路地赶着把大多数、最重的那批炮挪到临陆一面，重新安座，好迎候那随时可能从半英里外的树林里扑上来的进攻。
于是夜幕降临时，尽管对那伙海上以其亡命勇悍闻名的恶鬼的来袭怕得要死，西班牙人总算有了几分准备。他们守在炮位上等。

===Original===

And whilst they waited thus, under cover of the darkness and as the tide began to ebb, Captain Blood’s fleet weighed anchor quietly; and, as once before, with no more canvas spread than that which their sprits could carry, so as to give them steering way﻿—and even these having been painted black﻿—the four vessels, without a light showing, groped their way by soundings to the channel which led to that narrow passage out to sea.
The *Elizabeth* and the *Infanta*, leading side by side, were almost abreast of the fort before their shadowy bulks and the soft gurgle of water at their prows were detected by the Spaniards, whose attention until that moment had been all on the other side. And now there arose on the night air such a sound of human baffled fury as may have resounded about Babel at the confusion of tongues. To heighten that confusion, and to scatter disorder among the Spanish soldiery, the *Elizabeth* emptied her larboard guns into the fort as she was swept past on the swift ebb.
At once realizing﻿—though not yet how﻿—he had been duped, and that his prey was in the very act of escaping after all, the Admiral frantically ordered the guns that had been so laboriously moved to be dragged back to their former emplacements, and commanded his gunners meanwhile to the slender batteries that of all his powerful, but now unavailable, armament still remained trained upon the channel. With these, after the loss of some precious moments, the fort at last made fire.
It was answered by a terrific broadside from the *Arabella*, which had now drawn abreast, and was crowding canvas to her yards. The enraged and gibbering Spaniards had a brief vision of her as the line of flame spurted from her red flank, and the thunder of her broadside drowned the noise of the creaking halyards. After that they saw her no more. Assimilated by the friendly darkness which the lesser Spanish guns were speculatively stabbing, the escaping ships fired never another shot that might assist their baffled and bewildered enemies to locate them.
Some slight damage was sustained by Blood’s fleet. But by the time the Spaniards had resolved their confusion into some order of dangerous offence, that fleet, well served by a southerly breeze, was through the narrows and standing out to sea.

===Chinese===

他们正这么等着，趁着夜色、趁着退潮开始，布拉德船长的舰队悄悄起了锚；同先前那回一样，帆只张到斜桁撑得住的程度——为的只是舵效——连这点帆都漆成了黑色——四条船，不露一星灯火，靠测深索摸索着寻那通向出海窄道的水路。
“伊丽莎白号”（*Elizabeth*）同“因凡塔号”并肩领先，几乎驶过要塞了，那两团黑影和船头轻柔的湍水声才被西班牙人觉察——直到那一刻，他们的心思还全在另一头。随即夜空里腾起那样一种人气急败坏的怒吼，兴许当年变乱口音时巴别塔四下里响的就是这个。火上浇油，也叫西班牙军心更乱，“伊丽莎白号”乘着湍急落潮掠过要塞时，把左舷的炮倾泻进了炮台。
上将一时明白——尽管还不明白怎么上的当——自己被耍了，他的猎物终究正在脱手，登时疯狂地下令：把那千辛万苦挪走的炮拖回原位；又命炮手暂且奔向那几门稀拉的小炮——他强大却此刻动弹不得的火力里，仅剩的还对着水路的几门。损失了宝贵的几分钟之后，要塞终于用这几门开了火。
回敬它的，是此时已驶到正横、正朝桁上抢帆的“阿拉贝拉号”一阵雷霆般的舷炮。狂怒而语无伦次的西班牙人匆匆瞥了它一眼：一线火焰从它红色侧腹喷出，舷炮的轰鸣盖过了帆索吱嘎。此后他们再没见着它。融进友善的黑夜——西班牙那些较小的炮正在黑里没头没脑地乱戳——脱逃的船再没开过一炮，免得帮着受骗发懵的敌人给自己定位。
布拉德的舰队受了一点轻伤。可等西班牙人把混乱理出一点像样的攻击架势，那支舰队已借着南风过了窄道，出海去了。

===Original===

Thus was Don Miguel de Espinosa left to chew the bitter cud of a lost opportunity, and to consider in what terms he would acquaint the Supreme Council of the Catholic King that Peter Blood had got away from Maracaybo, taking with him two twenty-gun frigates that were lately the property of Spain, to say nothing of two hundred and fifty thousand pieces of eight and other plunder. And all this in spite of Don Miguel’s four galleons and his heavily armed fort that at one time had held the pirates so securely trapped.
Heavy, indeed, grew the account of Peter Blood, which Don Miguel swore passionately to Heaven should at all costs to himself be paid in full.
Nor were the losses already detailed the full total of those suffered on this occasion by the King of Spain. For on the following evening, off the coast of Oruba, at the mouth of the Gulf of Venezuela, Captain Blood’s fleet came upon the belated *Santo Niño*, speeding under full sail to reinforce Don Miguel at Maracaybo.
At first the Spaniard had conceived that she was meeting the victorious fleet of Don Miguel, returning from the destruction of the pirates. When at comparatively close quarters the pennon of St. George soared to the *Arabella*’s masthead to disillusion her, the *Santo Niño* chose the better part of valour, and struck her flag.
Captain Blood ordered her crew to take to the boats, and land themselves at Oruba or wherever else they pleased. So considerate was he that to assist them he presented them with several of the piraguas which he still had in tow.
“You will find,” said he to her captain, “that Don Miguel is in an extremely bad temper. Commend me to him, and say that I venture to remind him that he must blame himself for all the ills that have befallen him. The evil has recoiled upon him which he loosed when he sent his brother unofficially to make a raid upon the island of Barbados. Bid him think twice before he lets his devils loose upon an English settlement again.”
With that he dismissed the Captain, who went over the side of the *Santo Niño*, and Captain Blood proceeded to investigate the value of this further prize. When her hatches were removed, a human cargo was disclosed in her hold.
“Slaves,” said Wolverstone, and persisted in that belief cursing Spanish devilry until Cahusac crawled up out of the dark bowels of the ship, and stood blinking in the sunlight.

===Chinese===

就这样，堂米格尔·德·埃斯皮诺萨被撇在那里，嚼着坐失良机的苦草，盘算着该用什么措辞禀报天主教王的最高会议：彼得·布拉德从马拉开波脱身而去，带走两条二十炮的护卫舰——不久前还是西班牙的产业——更不必说二十五万块八里亚尔和其他赃物。而这一切，是在堂米格尔的四条大帆船和那座一度把海盗困得死死的重装要塞眼皮底下办成的。
彼得·布拉德欠账，账面越发沉重了；堂米格尔冲天起誓，不惜一切也要叫它分文不少地清偿。
西班牙国王这一回吃下的亏，明细之外还有添头。因为第二天傍晚，在奥鲁巴（Oruba）外海、委内瑞拉湾口，布拉德船长的舰队撞上了姗姗来迟的“圣尼诺号”——它正满帆疾驶，赶去马拉开波为堂米格尔增援。
起初，那西班牙船还当迎面来的是堂米格尔得胜的舰队，剿平了海盗凯旋。待到相距较近，圣乔治的燕尾旗窜上“阿拉贝拉号”的桅顶、破了它的迷梦，“圣尼诺号”识时务，降了旗。
布拉德船长命它的船员上小艇，去奥鲁巴或随便哪里自行上岸。他体恤到这个地步：为助他们一程，还把拖在船后的几条独木舟奉送了他们。
“你会发现，”他对它的船长说，“堂米格尔的火气大得很。替我问候他，并说：他遭的这一切祸，怪只怪他自己，我冒昧提醒他这一句。他打发他弟弟不官方地、私下里去袭巴巴多斯岛时放出去的恶，如今弹回了他自己身上。请他传话：再放他的魔鬼去碰英国人的殖民地处，先想三想。”
说完他打发那船长下了“圣尼诺号”的舷，自己着手清点这新一票的分量。舱盖揭开，货舱里露出人的货。
“奴隶。”伍尔夫斯通说完一口咬定，大骂西班牙的鬼伎俩——直到卡于萨克从漆黑的船腹里爬上来，站在日头底下眨眼。

===Original===

There was more than sunlight to make the Breton pirate blink. And those that crawled out after him﻿—the remnants of his crew﻿—cursed him horribly for the pusillanimity which had brought them into the ignominy of owing their deliverance to those whom they had deserted as lost beyond hope.
Their sloop had encountered and had been sunk three days ago by the *Santo Niño*, and Cahusac had narrowly escaped hanging merely that for some time he might be a mock among the Brethren of the Coast.
For many a month thereafter he was to hear in Tortuga the jeering taunt:
“Where do you spend the gold that you brought back from Maracaybo?”

===Chinese===

叫这布列塔尼海盗眨眼的，可不光是日头。跟着爬上来的那些人——他船员的残余——把他骂了个狗血淋头：他那贪生怕死的怂，害他们落到这步屈辱田地——救命恩人，竟是他们当作没救而撇下的那些人。
他们的单桅船三天前撞上“圣尼诺号”，被击沉了；卡于萨克险些吊死，逃下的这条命，只够他在海岸兄弟会中间当好一阵笑柄。
此后许多个月，他在托尔图加耳边老响着那句夾落：
“你从马拉开波带回来的金子，都花到哪儿去了？”

## XVIII The *Milagrosa* / 十八 米拉格罗萨号
===Original===

The affair at Maracaybo is to be considered as Captain Blood’s buccaneering masterpiece. Although there is scarcely one of the many actions that he fought﻿—recorded in such particular detail by Jeremy Pitt﻿—which does not afford some instance of his genius for naval tactics, yet in none is this more shiningly displayed than in those two engagements by which he won out of the trap which Don Miguel de Espinosa had sprung upon him.
The fame which he had enjoyed before this, great as it already was, is dwarfed into insignificance by the fame that followed. It was a fame such as no buccaneer﻿—not even Morgan﻿—has ever boasted, before or since.
In Tortuga, during the months he spent there refitting the three ships he had captured from the fleet that had gone out to destroy him, he found himself almost an object of worship in the eyes of the wild Brethren of the Coast, all of whom now clamoured for the honour of serving under him. It placed him in the rare position of being able to pick and choose the crews for his augmented fleet, and he chose fastidiously. When next he sailed away it was with a fleet of five fine ships in which went something over a thousand men. Thus you behold him not merely famous, but really formidable. The three captured Spanish vessels he had renamed with a certain scholarly humour the *Clotho*, *Lachesis*, and *Atropos*, a grimly jocular manner of conveying to the world that he made them the arbiters of the fate of any Spaniards he should henceforth encounter upon the seas.
In Europe the news of this fleet, following upon the news of the Spanish Admiral’s defeat at Maracaybo, produced something of a sensation. Spain and England were variously and unpleasantly exercised, and if you care to turn up the diplomatic correspondence exchanged on the subject, you will find that it is considerable and not always amiable.
And meanwhile in the Caribbean, the Spanish Admiral Don Miguel de Espinosa might be said﻿—to use a term not yet invented in his day﻿—to have run amok. The disgrace into which he had fallen as a result of the disasters suffered at the hands of Captain Blood had driven the Admiral all but mad. It is impossible, if we impose our minds impartially, to withhold a certain sympathy from Don Miguel. Hate was now this unfortunate man’s daily bread, and the hope of vengeance an obsession to his mind.

===Chinese===

马拉开波这一役，当视为布拉德船长海盗生涯的杰作。杰里米·皮特细致入微地记录下他打过的许多仗，几乎每一仗都能见出他海战天才的某个例证；但哪一仗也不如那两场接战来得耀眼——他凭着它们，从堂米格尔·德·埃斯皮诺萨扣下的陷阱里赢了出来。
他此前享有的名声，虽已不小，同随之而来的名声一比，便小得不足道了。那样的名声，前此此后，任何海盗——连摩根（Morgan）也算上——都不曾有过。
在托尔图加，在他花去数月、修整那三条从前来毁灭他的舰队手里俘来的船只的当口，他发觉自己在海岸弟兄们那些野汉子的眼里，几乎成了崇拜的对象，人人抢着要光荣地投到他的麾下。这使他处在一个难得的位置上：可以挑挑拣拣地为扩充的舰队选配人手，他也挑得极精。再度出航时，他麾下已是五条好船、一千余人的舰队。于是诸位看到，他不单有名，而且当真可畏。那三条俘来的西班牙船，他带着一点书卷气的幽默，改名为“克罗托号”（*Clotho*）、“拉刻西斯号”（*Lachesis*）和“阿特罗波斯号”（*Atropos*）——一种冷峭的谐谑，借以昭告世人：今后他在海上遇见的西班牙人，命运即由它们三位裁定。
在欧洲，这支舰队的消息，接在西班牙提督折戟马拉开波的消息之后，激起了一阵不小的轰动。西班牙和英格兰各自恼火，恼法不同，都不痛快；诸位若有兴致翻检为此事往来的外交函件，会发现数量可观，而且并不总是客客气气。
与此同时，在加勒比海，西班牙提督堂米格尔·德·埃斯皮诺萨可以说是——用一个他那时代还没造出来的词——疯了。败在布拉德船长手里蒙上的耻辱，把这提督逼得几乎发狂。我们只要持平地想一想，就不可能不给堂米格尔留几分同情。仇恨如今是这不幸者的日常口粮，复仇的希望是他心头驱不散的魔怔。

===Original===

As a madman he went raging up and down the Caribbean seeking his enemy, and in the meantime, as an hors d’oeuvre to his vindictive appetite, he fell upon any ship of England or of France that loomed above his horizon.
I need say no more to convey the fact that this illustrious sea-captain and great gentleman of Castile had lost his head, and was become a pirate in his turn. The Supreme Council of Castile might anon condemn him for his practices. But how should that matter to one who already was condemned beyond redemption? On the contrary, if he should live to lay the audacious and ineffable Blood by the heels, it was possible that Spain might view his present irregularities and earlier losses with a more lenient eye.
And so, reckless of the fact that Captain Blood was now in vastly superior strength, the Spaniard sought him up and down the trackless seas. But for a whole year he sought him vainly. The circumstances in which eventually they met are very curious.
An intelligent observation of the facts of human existence will reveal to shallow-minded folk who sneer at the use of coincidence in the arts of fiction and drama that life itself is little more than a series of coincidences. Open the history of the past at whatsoever page you will, and there you shall find coincidence at work bringing about events that the merest chance might have averted. Indeed, coincidence may be defined as the very tool used by Fate to shape the destinies of men and nations.
Observe it now at work in the affairs of Captain Blood and of some others.
On the 15th September of the year 1688﻿—a memorable year in the annals of England﻿—three ships were afloat upon the Caribbean, which in their coming conjunctions were to work out the fortunes of several persons.
The first of these was Captain Blood’s flagship the *Arabella*, which had been separated from the buccaneer fleet in a hurricane off the Lesser Antilles. In somewhere about 17° N. Lat., and 74° Long., she was beating up for the Windward Passage, before the intermittent southeasterly breezes of that stifling season, homing for Tortuga, the natural rendezvous of the dispersed vessels.
The second ship was the great Spanish galleon, the *Milagrosa*, which, accompanied by the smaller frigate *Hidalga*, lurked off the Caymites, to the north of the long peninsula that thrusts out from the southwest corner of Hispaniola.

===Chinese===

他像一个疯子，在加勒比海上暴跳着来去，四处寻他的仇人；与此同时，为给他的报复胃口添一道开胃小菜，凡在地平线上露头的英国船、法国船，见着便扑。
不必多说什么，诸位便明白：这位显赫的船长、卡斯蒂利亚的大绅士，已经失了方寸，自己也当起海盗来了。卡斯蒂利亚最高政务会早晚要办他的罪。可这对一个早已无可救药地定了罪的人，又算得了什么？相反，他若有朝一日擒住那个胆大包天、无以名状的布拉德，西班牙对他眼下的越轨和先前的折损，说不定反倒会另眼相看。
于是，全然不顾布拉德船长如今实力远胜于他，这西班牙人满无边的大洋上下寻他。可寻了整整一年，也没寻着。他们最终相遇的情形，十分蹊跷。
对人类生存的事实做一点有头脑的观察，便可以告诉那些讥诮小说戏剧里使用巧合的浅人们：生活本身，不过是接二连三的巧合。随便翻开过去的史册的哪一页，你都能看见巧合在那里起作用，促成着最微小的偶然本可避免的事件。的确，巧合不妨定义为命运用来雕琢个人与邦国前途的工具。
现在就来看它在布拉德船长和另外几位的事务中所起的作用。
一六八八年九月十五日——英格兰编年史上难忘的一年——加勒比海上漂着三条船，它们的交会将演出好几个人的命运。
第一条是布拉德船长的旗舰“阿拉贝拉号”，前此在小安的列斯群岛外的一场飓风里同海盗舰队失散了。在北纬约十七度、西经七十四度一带，它顶着那个闷热季节断断续续的东南风，抢风赶向向风海峡，回托尔图加去——失散船只的天然集合地。
第二条是西班牙大帆船“米拉格罗萨号”（*Milagrosa*），带着较小的巡航舰“伊达尔加号”（*Hidalga*），潜伏在开米特群岛外——在那道从伊斯帕尼奥拉西南角伸出来的长半岛以北。

===Original===

Aboard the *Milagrosa* sailed the vindictive Don Miguel.
The third and last of these ships with which we are at present concerned was an English man-of-war, which on the date I have given was at anchor in the French port of St. Nicholas on the northwest coast of Hispaniola. She was on her way from Plymouth to Jamaica, and carried on board a very distinguished passenger in the person of Lord Julian Wade, who came charged by his kinsman, my Lord Sunderland, with a mission of some consequence and delicacy, directly arising out of that vexatious correspondence between England and Spain.
The French Government, like the English, excessively annoyed by the depredations of the buccaneers, and the constant straining of relations with Spain that ensued, had sought in vain to put them down by enjoining the utmost severity against them upon her various overseas governors. But these, either﻿—like the Governor of Tortuga﻿—throve out of a scarcely tacit partnership with the filibusters, or﻿—like the Governor of French Hispaniola﻿—felt that they were to be encouraged as a check upon the power and greed of Spain, which might otherwise be exerted to the disadvantage of the colonies of other nations. They looked, indeed, with apprehension upon recourse to any vigorous measures which must result in driving many of the buccaneers to seek new hunting-grounds in the South Sea.
To satisfy King James’s anxiety to conciliate Spain, and in response to the Spanish Ambassador’s constant and grievous expostulations, my Lord Sunderland, the Secretary of State, had appointed a strong man to the deputy-governorship of Jamaica. This strong man was that Colonel Bishop who for some years now had been the most influential planter in Barbados.
Colonel Bishop had accepted the post, and departed from the plantations in which his great wealth was being amassed with an eagerness that had its roots in a desire to pay off a score of his own with Peter Blood.
From his first coming to Jamaica, Colonel Bishop had made himself felt by the buccaneers.

===Chinese===

“米拉格罗萨号”上坐镇的，正是那衔恨的堂米格尔。
与我们眼下有关的第三条、也是最后一条船，是一条英国军舰，在我所说的那日，泊在伊斯帕尼奥拉西北岸的法属港口圣尼古拉斯（St. Nicholas）。它正从普利茅斯驶往牙买加，船上载着一位身份煊赫的乘客——朱利安·韦德勋爵（Lord Julian Wade）；他受他的亲戚桑德兰勋爵委派，带着一桩关系不小、又须细心的使命，而这使命，正是直接从英西之间那场伤脑筋的函件往来里生出来的。
法国政府同英国政府一样，被海盗的劫掠和随之而来的对西关系的持续紧张烦透了，也曾训令各海外总督对他们严惩不贷，却是徒劳。这些总督，有的——如托尔图加总督——靠同劫掠者近乎心照不宣的合伙过肥日子；有的——如法属伊斯帕尼奥拉总督——觉得海盗们正该扶植，好掣肘西班牙的权势与贪欲，不然那权势与贪欲怕要拿来对付别国的殖民地。他们实在还忧心忡忡，生怕雷厉风行的措施会把大批海盗逼往南海另寻猎场。
为顺遂詹姆斯国王安抚西班牙之心，也为回应西班牙大使无休无止的苦口申诉，国务大臣桑德兰勋爵给牙买加副督的位置安了一个铁腕人物。这铁腕人物，就是这几年在巴巴多斯势力最大的种植园主、那位毕晓普上校。
毕晓普上校接了任，离开他那财源滚滚的种植园时那份急切，根子在一个念头：要同彼得·布拉德清一清私账。
打一到牙买加，毕晓普上校就叫海盗们感到了他的分量。

===Original===
